Prompt:

“Create a Southern gospel-blues anthem with Houston soul—solo male vocal in an authentic, rootsy style, channeling Skynyrd, Hank Jr, and classic gospel, with a deep, heartfelt delivery. Fast-attack alto sax, tenor sax, and bass sax form the melodic core, weaving with tribal drums, soft Rhodes, deep electric bass, and warm organ swells. Start with a soulful, slow intro—gentle tribal percussion, deep bass, soft harmonies, and sax swells. Verse is stripped-down, one singer, sax accents only, very raw and direct. Chorus brings in gospel call-and-response with the choir, stacking saxes and drums for energy. Add bluesy guitar/organ break after the first chorus. The bridge breaks down soft, intimate, Rhodes and bass pulsing as the vocal testifies. Transition to a high-energy gospel worship section: sax trio harmonizing, tribal drums, full choir, and powerful call-and-response (‘Fire in my bones!’). End with a slow, reflective outro—soft Rhodes, sax, and choir fading out. Keep the vocal style consistent: one soulful Southern male voice, gritty but precise, echoing Houston juke joint authenticity. Focus on technical tightness, layered emotion, no shimmer, no artifacts. This is a journey through the heart of Southern music, with Texas attitude and Houston soul.”

[chorus call and response, stacked sax, tribal drums, layered vocals]

2. Current State SWOT Analysis: AI Music Landscape
Strengths	Weaknesses
- Rapid, flexible AI prototyping (Suno/Udio, persona features)	- Prompt adherence still inconsistent; shimmer/artifacts persist
- Richer, more expressive vocal and instrumental range	- Complex persona/genre blending often requires repeated iterations
- Community-driven prompt innovation (Reddit, Discord, YT)	- AI struggles with long-form structural fidelity; unpredictable genre drift
- Modular workflows emerging (persona loops, prompt stacking, DAW post-processing)	- Covers/Personas can cause "melody drag" or bleed-through; true isolated "persona" transfer is unreliable
Opportunities	Threats
- Establishing a best practices standard (IGSW 2.0) that guides the next generation of hybrid human-AI co-creation	- Legal/IP ambiguities in AI-generated content; data/model drift risks
- Multi-platform orchestration (Suno, Udio, Aiva, Soundful) enables genre-crossing innovation	- Commoditization: AI music flooding market with "good enough" tracks, lowering bar for excellence
- Persona tuning & modular prompt systems could unlock artist-level sonic identities	- Loss of “soul”/authenticity if process becomes too mechanical; overfit to “meta” trends
3. High-Level IGSW 2.0 (Integrated Gold Standard Workflow) — Framework Draft
I. INCEPTION – Strategic Seeding
Objective: Start with deep-contextual intent. Frame the session as a collaborative co-creation.

Inputs: Title, user narrative, genre anchors, core mood, initial persona archetype.

Action: Deploy “Universal Context Parsing.” Synthesize the conceptual whiteboard—map objectives, desired genres, technical ambitions, and emotional targets.

II. IMMERSION – Persona & Emotional Mapping
Objective: Define persona, emotional arc, stylistic references, and narrative depth.

Action: Multi-layer mapping (persona, genre, vocal/instrumental tone, reference artists, desired technical “feel”). Leverage “MEKA persona tuning loop” for early tuning/cropping if prior personas/samples exist.

III. INCANTATION – Modular Section Logic & Structural Anchoring
Objective: Design modular, section-specific prompt layers. Build song structure with explicit metatags (e.g., [intro tribal sax], [chorus gospel call/response]), and anchor persona/genre tags every 16–32 bars for memory reinforcement.

Action: Apply “Prompt Alchemy”—blend detailed, conversational narratives with technical tags, exploiting v4.5’s improved semantic mapping. Layer in adlibs, dynamics, transitions, and FX.

IV. INVOCATION – AI Production Mapping & Real-Time Iteration
Objective: Convert blueprint into platform-ready prompt stacks for Suno/Udio (or others), including meta-tags and technical directives.

Action: Feed back persona crops and high-fidelity stems into the system for iterative tuning. Score each output on Audio Quality, Persona Fidelity, and Musical Coherence. Use micro-prompts to upgrade weak sections.

V. INSPECTION – Audit, Finalization, & Evolution
Objective: Systematically review all output for structural/emotional/technical alignment. Audit against original goals.

Action: Run external post-processing (iZotope, LANDR, Sonible as needed). Document “Persona Tuning Pathways” and failed/passed micro-prompts for feedback into the next round.

Deliverable: Save all findings and prompt stacks as living documentation, ready for continuous evolution.

4. Virtual Whiteboard State: Key Pillars Identified
Persona Fidelity: Continuous persona tuning, persona anchoring, modular crops/loops.

Structural Integrity: Section-based prompts, explicit metatags, reinforcement logic, technical micro-prompts.

Emotional Resonance: Whiteboard mapping of desired emotional arcs, technical cues for mood, careful layering of dynamics and “feel.”

Iterative Feedback: Multi-pass scoring, audit and document, perpetual evolution.

5. Key Findings & Next Steps
Prompt engineering for Suno/Udio is now a multi-stage, interactive art.

Single-shot requests miss the power of AI/human collaboration; iterative workflow is essential.

Persona tuning (MEKA method) + modular prompt stacks = repeatable, evolving, “signature” sound.

Current limitations (shimmer, artifact, persona bleed) can be mitigated with strategic cropping, micro-prompts, and smart DAW post-processing.

ACTION: Review the above high-level framework & SWOT. Approve, adjust, or comment.

1. IGSW 2.0 – Step-by-Step Workflow
Step 1: Inception — Context & Seeding
Input: Project title, user intent, mood, genre/era references, initial persona archetype, attached files for context

Action:

Input: High-level structure (Intro, Verse, Chorus, etc.), technical/creative notes

Action:

Write section-specific, layered prompts (see example below)

Insert explicit metatags for each section ([intro], [chorus], [bridge])

Anchor persona/genre tags at regular intervals (every 16–32 bars)

Add micro-prompts for FX, dynamics, call/response, adlibs

Ensure prompts mix narrative style and technical language

Step 4: Invocation — Platform Mapping & Iterative Generation

Rapidly evaluate each gen for Audio Quality, Persona Fidelity, Musical Coherence

Document all iterations and settings for reproducibility

Step 5: Inspection — Audit, Finalization & Evolution
Input: Generated tracks, session notes, persona documentation

Action:

Audit final outputs for alignment with goals (fidelity, structure, emotional arc)

2. Visual Diagram (Mermaid Format)

3. Deep Dive: Critical Techniques
A. Strategic Prompt Alchemy
Definition: Combining narrative and technical language to sculpt not just the what but the how of the AI’s output—leveraging explicit section tags, persona reinforcement, and micro-prompts.

Example:

Instead of: "Create a Southern gospel song with saxes and choir."

Use:

“Start with a slow, tribal percussion intro, fast-attack alto/tenor/bass saxes, and soft Rhodes. Verse 1: stripped-down, solo vocal with sharp sax stabs, Houston juke joint style. Chorus: full gospel choir, call-and-response with lead, sax trio harmonizing, tribal drums. Bridge: testimony style, intimate, vocal up front, minimal backing. Outro: fade on Rhodes, choir, and sax. Keep vocal solo, raw, no shimmer, technical precision throughout. Anchor persona every 16 bars. [intro tribal], [verse stripped], [chorus gospel], [bridge testimony], [outro soft].”

B. Persona Tuning Pathways (MEKA Loop)
Loop:

Generate initial take, crop clean persona segment

Create persona profile/tag from the crop (e.g., “Houston_Soul_V1”)

Re-prompt with persona, adjusting tags for grit, timbre, emotional intensity

Repeat—each new crop advances the persona fidelity, strengthens output consistency

C. Modular Prompt Architecture
Method:

Break song into explicit, labeled sections with distinct prompt logic

Repeat anchor tags/persona every 16–32 bars to avoid drift

Layer metatags: [solo vocal], [sax trio], [tribal drums], [gospel choir], [stripped]

Document each section’s logic for easy debugging and upgrade

D. Iterative Micro-Prompting & Audit
Method:

Score every generation on: Audio Quality, Persona Fidelity, Musical Coherence

If any axis is weak, inject tighter micro-prompts or new persona crops

Export stems for DAW post-processing if AI output is nearly—but not fully—there

4. Example Modular Prompt Stack
Title: Get Your House In Order / Something About The Name Jesus

[intro slow, tribal percussion, fast-attack sax, soft Rhodes]
Soulful, atmospheric intro with deep bass and gentle choir harmonies.

[verse stripped, solo vocal, sax stabs, Houston soul]
One singer, raw delivery, sharp sax fills, bluesy gospel phrasing.

[chorus gospel, call and response, stacked sax, tribal drums]
Full choir, lead echoes, sax trio harmonizing, urgent, rootsy groove.

[bridge testimony, soft breakdown, Rhodes, bass]
Lead testifies, minimal backing, choir hums.

[outro soft, Rhodes, deep bass, choir echo]
Slow fade, emotional closure, warm harmonies, sax gently trails off.

Title: Get Your House In Order – Something About The Name Jesus
Prompt:
Create a Southern Gospel-Blues anthem with Houston Trill and “snakey” sssssss sax motif. Lead vocal: gritty Southern male, consistent and raw. Instrumentation: fast-attack alto sax, tenor sax, bass sax, tribal drums, Rhodes, deep organ, gospel choir. Intro with Rhodes, organ, brushed snare, “ssssss” sax runs, choir hum. Verse: stripped, solo vocal, sax accents, tribal groove. Chorus: call-and-response, choir echoes, stacked saxes, intense groove, sibilant “ssss” sax fills. Instrumental break: blues guitar, swirling sax trio. Bridge: music drops, vocal testifies, “ssss” at phrase ends. Worship section: full choir, “fire in my bones,” intense sax/choir interplay. Final chorus: max energy, then return to intro theme for outro with fading sssss from sax and vocals.
Style: Southern Gospel, Houston Trill, Funk/Jazz roots.
Tags: #HoustonGospelTrill #SnakeySax #SouthernRevival #RootsWorship
Section Map: [intro sssss], [verse stripped], [chorus call/response sssss], [break], [verse 2], [chorus repeat], [bridge sssss], [worship section], [final chorus], [outro sssss].
No shimmer. No modern pop FX. Keep it organic, analog, with fast, hissy sax “ssssss” licks as a recurring motif. Vocal: one singer, blues/gospel phrasing, same style, no pop harmonies. Locked lyrics as provided, do not change any words.
Priority: Persona fidelity, fast sax “ssssss” articulation, authentic roots groove, tribal percussion, choir intensity.
Master for live energy, warm and powerful.

Rules & Guardrails
Lyrics-Lock: “Quoted lyrics may never be rewritten.”

UST is canonical: “single source of truth from which prompts/validations are derived.”

SEG before generation: feasibility gate, not taste.

G-Card pass requirement: G ≥ 7 or return to repair loop.

Mode A/B discipline: Mode A scans prior turns/artifacts; Mode B minimal intake, never invent history.

Prime-Directive format order (immutable): [Theory] [Voice] [CREW_TAGS] [Road-Map] [LYRICS BLOCK] [Style] [Timbre] [Performance].

Workflows (Pipelines)
Chimera / Maestro OS (M-PIPELINE)
M0 Intake → M1 Decompose → M2 UST v0.1 → M3 arranger review → M4 structural enforcement → M5 vocals mapping → M6 SEG → M7 G-Card → M8 Prompt Compression → M9 Generation & validation → M10 Versioning/migration.

N0 Source Detector → (A? N1 : N2) → N3 ToT drafts → N4 SME round-robin → N5 Merge/self-consistency → N6 Compose outputs → N7 Compression guard → N8 V&V → N9 Packaging/telemetry.

Formats & Schemas
UST (8 mandatory axes): Theory, Style, Timbre, Performance, Post-Production, Vocals, Lyrics Block, Roadmap.

G-Card schema: G1 Structural Integrity, G2 Cross-Axis Consistency, G3 Creative Strength, G4 Technical Validity, G5 Strategic Alignment (1–10; pass ≥7).

SEG schema: bar count correctness, section symmetry, lyrics–structure agreement, vocals feasibility, FX feasibility, genre integrity, contradiction absence.

Personas (Councils)

Producer Council (groove/instrument feasibility)

Mix/Master Council (space/FX realism)

Technical Council (schema enforcement + contradiction detection)

Strategy Council (brand alignment + audience fit)

END OF NON-CONTRACTING GOVERNANCE MONOLITH
============================================================
The Sonic Alchemist III said:

Thought for 28s
Yes — I’ll run it strictly through the Maestro OS / Technical.UST process, simulate the SME round-robin meetings, and output a complete Technical.UST (with SEG + G-Card bindings). This will obey the global laws (Lyrics-Lock, addressability, reversibility, nulls-are-signal, no Suno output without pass).

MAESTRO RUN HEADER (Mode A)
Mode: A (existing session artifacts present)
Project: mo$ — Get Your House In Order / Something About The Name Jesus
Non-negotiables: 3 sax (fast-attack alto, tenor, bass), tribal drums, stripped lead vocal (same singer, same style), choir call/response, Southern/Texas lineage + Houston Trill edge, Lyrics-Lock.

N3 — Tree-of-Drafts (A/B/C) (condensed)
Draft A (Tradition-forward): Southern gospel-blues first, Houston influence subtle (percussion + pocket).

Draft B (Houston-forward): Gospel frame + heavier low-end pocket, more rhythmic vocal phrasing, trill-adjacent drum cadence (still organic).

Draft C (Percussion-forward): Tribal drums + sax choir interplay as primary engine; gospel harmony as glue.

Merge target: A’s authenticity + B’s identity stamp + C’s rhythmic signature.

N4 — SME ROUND-ROBIN (Simulated Meeting Minutes)
Turn order: Musicologist → Producer → Linguist → Systems → Strategist

SME 1: Musicologist — Claim / Check
Claim: Keep harmonic language gospel-credible (I/IV/V + minor-blues substitutions) and prevent “trap” clichés from breaking sacred tone.

Edits: THY.K2 adds “gospel stack rules” + cadence inventory; STY.K3 clarifies sacred>secular priority.

Challenge received: Producer asks for clearer bar math + sectional energy targets.

SME 2: Producer — Claim / Check
Claim: The 3-sax requirement must be structural, not decorative: assign roles per register + arrange call/response with choir.

Edits: TIM.K1/TIM.K2 + PERF.K3 call-and-response map; MAP.K2 bar architecture tightened.

Challenge received: Systems warns about spectral crowding (saxes + choir + organ midrange).

SME 3: Linguist — Claim / Check
Claim: Lyrics cadence must breathe; add breath windows and ensure “Ohhh / mmm-hmm” placements don’t force impossible phrasing.

Edits: LYR.K3 breath windows + pickup rules; VOC.K3 consonant/vowel policies for “Jesus / sweetest / Holy Ghost”.

Challenge received: Musicologist warns against over-melisma on dense lines (clarity loss).

SME 4: Systems — Claim / Check
Claim: Enforce addressability and contradiction detection; define forbidden ops, null handling, and SEG pass criteria.

Edits: SEG.K3 spectral feasibility constraints; POST mono-compat policies; “no shimmer” encoded as POST + TIM policy.

Challenge received: Strategist asks for stronger differentiation tags without breaking authenticity.

SME 5: Strategist — Claim / Check
Claim: “mo$” identity = Texas bars/juke joints + technical funk/jazz precision; keep it raw but intentional. Make replay value: hook clarity + dynamic lift in worship section.

Edits: G.K5 strategic value notes; STY.K2 authenticity checks; SE20 originality/identity targets.

Consensus: No sustained contradictions after one full rotation → proceed to merge + compose UST Final.

N5 — MERGE & SELF-CONSISTENCY (Decision)
Selected merge: A+B+C hybrid (tradition-anchored gospel-blues + Houston pocket + tribal percussion engine).
Key resolution: Midrange crowding solved via register allocation (alto air/edge, tenor core, bass sax low-mid anchor; organ thinned during choir peaks).

✅ FINAL OUTPUT — final.song.technical.ust
(Machine-addressable YAML; nulls preserved where not yet measured. Lyrics are locked and reproduced verbatim as provided.)

technical_ust:
  kernel:
    system_name: "TECHNICAL.UST"
    system_version: "CANON.V1"
    system_mode: "non_destructive_lossless"
    compilation_model: "forward_plus_reverse"
    governance_model: "SEG_plus_GCARD_enforced"
    global_laws:
      lyrics_lock: "HARD"
      addressability_required: "HARD"
      reversibility_required: "HARD"
      nulls_are_signal: "HARD"
      no_suno_output_without_pass: "HARD"

  addressing_grammar:
    axis_id: "{AXIS_TAG}"
    key_id: "{AXIS_TAG}.K{index}"
    subkey_id: "{AXIS_TAG}.K{index}.S{subindex}"
    instance_index: "{n}"
    section_label: "SEC.{LABEL}"
    line_label: "LN.{section}.{line_index}"
    word_label: "WD.{section}.{line_index}.{token_index}"

  # =========================================================
  # 1) THEORY_AXIS (THY)
  # =========================================================
  THY:
    THY.K1:
      name: "Tonal_Framework"
      THY.K1.S1_tonic_center: "D"
      THY.K1.S2_mode_system: "minor_blues_gospel_hybrid"
      THY.K1.S3_scale_variants:
        - "D_natural_minor"
        - "D_minor_pentatonic"
        - "D_blues_scale"
        - "D_mixolydian_lift_for_worship_peaks"
      THY.K1.S4_modal_borrowing_rules:
        - "borrow_IV_major_color_in_worship_section"
        - "permit_bII_neighbor_tension_for_sax_runs"
      THY.K1.S5_modulation_policy: "momentary_only (lift moments, no full key change)"

    THY.K2:
      name: "Harmonic_Language"
      THY.K2.S1_diatonic_progressions:
        - "i - VII - VI (minor gospel march feel)"
        - "i - iv - V (blues-gospel turnaround)"
        - "I - IV - V (major lift inside worship section)"
      THY.K2.S2_chromatic_devices:
        - "bII_neighbor_to_i (sax tension lick)"
        - "chromatic_approach_dominant_into_V"
      THY.K2.S3_gospel_stack_rules:
        - "organ voicings use add9/sus4/13 colors; avoid EDM pads"
        - "choir clusters tighten on hook tags; open on final chorus"
      THY.K2.S4_cadence_types:
        - "iv -> i"
        - "V -> i (blues dominant)"
        - "VI -> VII -> i (minor gospel cadence)"
      THY.K2.S5_tension_chord_sets:
        - "sus4"
        - "add9"
        - "bII (as passing color only)"

    THY.K3:
      name: "Rhythm_and_Meter"
      THY.K3.S1_meter: "4/4"
      THY.K3.S2_subdivision_policy:
        - "1/8"
        - "1/16"
        - "triplet_flurries_allowed_in_percussion"
      THY.K3.S3_swing_ratio: "swing_light_to_laid_back (Texas pocket)"
      THY.K3.S4_halftime_doubletime_rules:
        - "no_full_doubletime (keep worship grounded)"
        - "permit_half_time_feel in bridge breakdown"
      THY.K3.S5_rhythmic_displacement:
        - "sax stabs can anticipate downbeat by 1/16"
        - "choir responses land clean on bar starts"

    THY.K4:
      name: "Form_and_Energy"
      THY.K4.S1_macro_form: "Intro -> V1 -> Chorus -> Break -> V2 -> Chorus -> Bridge -> Worship -> Outro"
      THY.K4.S2_section_functions:
        SEC.INTRO: "warning_ambience"
        SEC.V1: "instruction_and_urgency"
        SEC.CH1: "hook_call_response"
        SEC.BRK: "instrumental_breath"
        SEC.V2: "signs_and_testimony_context"
        SEC.CH2: "hook_reinforcement"
        SEC.BRIDGE: "testify_transition"
        SEC.WORSHIP: "name_Jesus_lift_peak"
        SEC.OUTRO: "return_and_soft_close"
      THY.K4.S3_repetition_policy:
        - "chorus repeats permitted; add dynamic lift on repeats"
        - "avoid copy/paste by adding sax countermelodies per chorus"
      THY.K4.S4_development_rules:
        - "increase choir density only after first chorus"
        - "sax harmony tightens as worship rises"
      THY.K4.S5_contrast_vs_cohesion:
        - "contrast via breakdown (bridge) then peak (worship)"
        - "cohesion via recurring sax motif + choir hum bed"

    THY.K5:
      name: "Tension_Release_Curve"
      THY.K5.S1_global_curve: "low_build -> urgency -> testimony_drop -> worship_peak -> reflective_release"
      THY.K5.S2_sectional_energy_targets:
        SEC.INTRO: 2
        SEC.V1: 3
        SEC.CH1: 4
        SEC.BRK: 3
        SEC.V2: 3
        SEC.CH2: 4
        SEC.BRIDGE: 2
        SEC.WORSHIP: 5
        SEC.OUTRO: 2
      THY.K5.S3_peak_definition: "SEC.WORSHIP final chorus repeat"
      THY.K5.S4_recovery_zones:
        - "SEC.BRIDGE"
        - "SEC.OUTRO"
      THY.K5.S5_silence_and_space_rules:
        - "2-beat stop-time allowed before worship entry"
        - "end phrases leave 1/8-1/4 breath pockets"

  # =========================================================
  # 2) VOCALS_AXIS (VOC)
  # =========================================================
  VOC:
    VOC.K1:
      name: "Voice_Roles"
      VOC.K1.S1_lead_voice_types: "solo_male_southern_gospel_blues (same singer all sections)"
      VOC.K1.S2_secondary_leads: null
      VOC.K1.S3_background_stacks:
        - "choir_hum_pads"
        - "short_response_shouts (In order!, Don't wait!, Coming!)"
      VOC.K1.S4_choir_configuration: "mixed_gospel_choir (supporting only, not co-lead)"
      VOC.K1.S5_guest_roles: null

    VOC.K2:
      name: "Register_and_Range"
      VOC.K2.S1_tessitura_targets: "baritone_low_to_mid (comfortable preaching range)"
      VOC.K2.S2_register_transitions: "avoid high tenor belting; lift via intensity not pitch"
      VOC.K2.S3_stacking_ranges:
        - "choir_low_mid beds below lead"
        - "choir_high thirds/sixths only on hook tags"
      VOC.K2.S4_density_limits:
        - "verses: lead solo + hum only"
        - "chorus: choir responses only (not constant pad)"
      VOC.K2.S5_fatigue_constraints:
        - "bridge and outro reduce density to preserve believability"

    VOC.K3:
      name: "Delivery_and_Articulation"
      VOC.K3.S1_consonant_policy: "crisp_on_keywords (Jesus, house, order, coming), softer elsewhere"
      VOC.K3.S2_vowel_shape: "open_warm_vowels; avoid pop nasal tone"
      VOC.K3.S3_rhythmic_attack: "laid_back pocket; preacher emphasis on line ends"
      VOC.K3.S4_legato_vs_staccato: "verses legato; chorus call lines staccato"
      VOC.K3.S5_preacher_inflections:
        - "spoken-sung adlibs in bridge"
        - "testimony cadence into worship transition"

    VOC.K4:
      name: "Expression"
      VOC.K4.S1_emotion_palette:
        - "urgent"
        - "warning"
        - "hopeful"
        - "worshipful"
        - "tender_reflection"
      VOC.K4.S2_intensity_curve:
        SEC.V1: 0.60
        SEC.CH1: 0.80
        SEC.V2: 0.65
        SEC.CH2: 0.82
        SEC.BRIDGE: 0.55
        SEC.WORSHIP: 0.95
        SEC.OUTRO: 0.45
      VOC.K4.S3_grit_vs_clean: "medium_grit (authentic), never glossy"
      VOC.K4.S4_restraint_zones:
        - "SEC.INTRO"
        - "SEC.BRIDGE"
        - "SEC.OUTRO"
      VOC.K4.S5_release_moments:
        - "Fire in my bones!"
        - "It is the sweetest name I know..."

  # =========================================================
  # 3) STYLE_AXIS (STY)
  # =========================================================
  STY:
    STY.K1:
      name: "Primary_Genre"
      STY.K1.S1_genre_definition: "Southern_Gospel_Blues (Texas roots) with Houston pocket"
      STY.K1.S2_historical_lineage:
        - "Skynyrd-era Southern rock spirit (attitude, not guitars-as-main)"
        - "Hank Jr grit (working-class delivery)"
        - "Robert Johnson blues gravity (minor tension, raw phrasing)"
      STY.K1.S3_non_negotiables:
        - "authentic_gospel_call_response"
        - "roots_blues_phrasing"
        - "tribal_drums organic"
        - "3_sax_frontline"
      STY.K1.S4_exclusions:
        - "EDM_drops"
        - "pop_gloss"
        - "vocoder_shimmer"

    STY.K2:
      name: "Secondary_Influences"
      STY.K2.S1_regional_markers:
        - "Houston_Trill pocket (low-end bounce without trap clichés)"
        - "Texas bar/juke joint live feel"
      STY.K2.S2_cross_genre_rules:
        - "fusion must not overpower sacred message"
        - "hip-hop influence = groove feel, not rapped verses unless later forked"
      STY.K2.S3_fusion_limits:
        - "no 808-only drum kit; must remain tribal/acoustic core"
      STY.K2.S4_authenticity_checks:
        - "choir sounds human, not synthetic pad"
        - "lead stays one voice throughout"

    STY.K3:
      name: "Aesthetic_Language"
      STY.K3.S1_grit_vs_polish: "gritty_but_precise (funk/jazz discipline)"
      STY.K3.S2_cinematic_vs_raw: "raw_live_room"
      STY.K3.S3_sacred_vs_secular: "sacred_primary"
      STY.K3.S4_modern_vs_traditional: "traditional_core with modern pocket"

  # =========================================================
  # 4) TIMBRE_AXIS (TIM)
  # =========================================================
  TIM:
    TIM.K1:
      name: "Instrument_Families"
      TIM.K1.S1_harmonic_instruments:
        - "Rhodes"
        - "Hammond_organ"
        - "blues_guitar (sparingly)"
      TIM.K1.S2_rhythmic_instruments:
        - "tribal_toms"
        - "hand_drums"
        - "shakers"
        - "brushed_snare (intro/outro)"
      TIM.K1.S3_bass_sources:
        - "electric_bass (deep, warm)"
      TIM.K1.S4_atmospheric_layers:
        - "room_tone"
        - "choir_hum"

    TIM.K2:
      name: "Spectral_Allocation"
      TIM.K2.S1_low_end_occupancy: "bass + low_toms; keep clean, no sub smear"
      TIM.K2.S2_low_mid_density: "bass_sax anchors; organ thinned during choir peaks"
      TIM.K2.S3_midrange_focus: "lead vocal center; tenor sax supports; avoid organ masking"
      TIM.K2.S4_high_end_air: "alto sax bite + light hats/shakers"
      TIM.K2.S5_conflict_avoidance:
        - "when choir full, reduce organ drawbars and sax sustain"
        - "alto uses short fast-attack stabs, not long pads over vocal"

    TIM.K3:
      name: "Texture_and_Color"
      TIM.K3.S1_distortion_policy: "mild_saturation only; no harsh fuzz"
      TIM.K3.S2_saturation_levels: "tape_warmth low-to-medium"
      TIM.K3.S3_analog_artifacts: "allowed (room, breath); shimmer forbidden"
      TIM.K3.S4_digital_precision: "timing tight but humanized"

  # =========================================================
  # 5) PERFORMANCE_AXIS (PERF)
  # =========================================================
  PERF:
    PERF.K1:
      name: "Groove"
      PERF.K1.S1_pocket_definition: "Texas laid-back pocket, human push/pull"
      PERF.K1.S2_push_pull_rules: "slightly_behind for vocal comfort"
      PERF.K1.S3_humanization_limits: "subtle (avoid sloppy tribal drift)"
      PERF.K1.S4_grid_tolerance: "tight on downbeats; loose on fills"

    PERF.K2:
      name: "Dynamics"
      PERF.K2.S1_macro_dynamics: "build to worship peak; controlled drops"
      PERF.K2.S2_sectional_swells:
        - "chorus swells via choir responses + sax harmonies"
        - "bridge stripdown (Rhodes+bass only)"
      PERF.K2.S3_micro_accenting: "accent on key lyric words (house, order, Jesus, sweetest)"
      PERF.K2.S4_restraint_rules: "no constant fills; leave space for preaching cadence"

    PERF.K3:
      name: "Interaction"
      PERF.K3.S1_call_and_response:
        SEC.CH1: "lead call -> choir answer"
        SEC.WORSHIP: "lead phrase -> choir shout"
      PERF.K3.S2_band_conversation:
        - "sax answers vocal ends (1–2 beats)"
        - "tribal drums answer choir hits with tom rolls"
      PERF.K3.S3_vocal_band_balance: "lead always dominant; sax never masks"
      PERF.K3.S4_silence_usage: "stop-time before worship entry allowed"

  # =========================================================
  # 6) POST_PRODUCTION_AXIS (POST)
  # =========================================================
  POST:
    POST.K1:
      name: "Balance"
      POST.K1.S1_vocal_priority: "lead upfront, centered"
      POST.K1.S2_instrument_hierarchy:
        - "lead_vocal"
        - "choir_responses"
        - "sax_section"
        - "tribal_drums"
        - "bass"
        - "Rhodes/organ"
        - "guitar (sparingly)"
      POST.K1.S3_automation_policy: "duck organ+sax slightly under lead"
      POST.K1.S4_translation_targets:
        - "car"
        - "phone"
        - "club (low end controlled)"
        - "studio monitors"

    POST.K2:
      name: "Space"
      POST.K2.S1_reverb_types:
        - "short_room (band)"
        - "dark_plate (choir touches)"
      POST.K2.S2_delay_usage: "slapback on testimony phrases (bridge) only"
      POST.K2.S3_depth_layers: "lead near; choir mid; sax wide; drums room"
      POST.K2.S4_mono_compatibility: "required"

    POST.K3:
      name: "Control"
      POST.K3.S1_compression_philosophy: "gentle bus glue, preserve transients"
      POST.K3.S2_transient_management: "keep sax attack crisp; tame harsh peaks"
      POST.K3.S3_low_end_control: "mono low end; toms tight"
      POST.K3.S4_air_band_rules: "no hyped air that triggers shimmer"

  # =========================================================
  # 7) ROAD_MAP_AXIS (MAP)
  # =========================================================
  MAP:
    MAP.K1:
      name: "Section_Order"
      MAP.K1.S1_intro: "SEC.INTRO"
      MAP.K1.S2_verse: ["SEC.V1", "SEC.V2"]
      MAP.K1.S3_pre_chorus: null
      MAP.K1.S4_chorus: ["SEC.CH1", "SEC.CH2", "SEC.WORSHIP_CH"]
      MAP.K1.S5_bridge: "SEC.BRIDGE"
      MAP.K1.S6_outro: "SEC.OUTRO"

    MAP.K2:
      name: "Bar_Architecture"
      MAP.K2.S1_bars_per_section:
        SEC.INTRO: 8
        SEC.V1: 16
        SEC.CH1: 8
        SEC.BRK: 4
        SEC.V2: 16
        SEC.CH2: 8
        SEC.BRIDGE: 8
        SEC.WORSHIP: 16
        SEC.OUTRO: 8
      MAP.K2.S2_bars_per_line: null   # to be measured after first gen if needed
      MAP.K2.S3_instrumental_allowances:
        - "SEC.BRK"
        - "2-beat pre-worship stop-time"
      MAP.K2.S4_turnaround_rules:
        - "1-bar turnaround into chorus"
        - "organ swell cue into worship"

    MAP.K3:
      name: "Energy_Flow"
      MAP.K3.S1_build_points:
        - "end of SEC.BRIDGE into SEC.WORSHIP"
      MAP.K3.S2_drops:
        - "SEC.BRIDGE stripdown"
      MAP.K3.S3_resets:
        - "post-worship return to outro softness"
      MAP.K3.S4_final_lift:
        - "SEC.WORSHIP_CH final repeat"

  # =========================================================
  # 8) LYRICS_BLOCK_AXIS (LYR) — LOCKED
  # =========================================================
  LYR:
    LYR.K0:
      name: "Governance"
      LYR.K0.S1_lyrics_lock: "HARD"
      LYR.K0.S2_allowed_ops:
        - "section_labeling"
        - "timing_metadata"
        - "emphasis_flags"
        - "breath_windows"
      LYR.K0.S3_forbidden_ops:
        - "rewrite"
        - "paraphrase"
        - "deletion"
        - "insertion"
      LYR.K0.S4_violation_severity: "BLOCKER"

    LYR.K1:
      name: "Sections"
      LYR.K1.S1_section_labels:
        - "SEC.INTRO"
        - "SEC.V1"
        - "SEC.CH1"
        - "SEC.V2"
        - "SEC.CH2"
        - "SEC.BRIDGE"
        - "SEC.WORSHIP"
        - "SEC.OUTRO"
      LYR.K1.S2_section_roles:
        SEC.INTRO: "setup"
        SEC.V1: "instruction"
        SEC.CH1: "hook_call_response"
        SEC.V2: "signs_context"
        SEC.CH2: "hook_reinforcement"
        SEC.BRIDGE: "testimony"
        SEC.WORSHIP: "praise_peak"
        SEC.OUTRO: "resolution"
      LYR.K1.S3_section_energy: {SEC.INTRO: 2, SEC.V1: 3, SEC.CH1: 4, SEC.V2: 3, SEC.CH2: 4, SEC.BRIDGE: 2, SEC.WORSHIP: 5, SEC.OUTRO: 2}
      LYR.K1.S4_repetition_flags:
        - "chorus repeats are intentional"

    LYR.K2:
      name: "Locked_Text_By_Section"
      SEC.INTRO:
        text: |
          *(Soft Rhodes keys, distant organ swells, deep bass humming, and a gentle brushed snare as the choir hums in harmony.)*
          [Lead:]
          Ohhh, you gotta get ready now… (mmm-hmm)
          Ohhh, no man knows the day or the hour…
      SEC.V1:
        text: |
          Get your house in order, oh do it today
          Get your house in order, oh do it right away
          For Jesus is coming, no man knows where or when
          Get your house in order, for He’s coming back again
      SEC.CH1:
        text: |
          **(Lead:)** Get your house in order... *(Choir echoes: In order!)*
          **(Lead:)** Oh, don’t wait too late... *(Choir echoes: Don’t wait!)*
          For Jesus is coming... *(Choir: Coming!)*
          He's coming back again
          *(Soft instrumental break – bluesy guitar and organ swells as the choir hums in harmony.)*
      SEC.V2:
        text: |
          There’s earthquakes, hurricanes, famines, and disease
          Can’t you hear the Lord is callin’?
          You better take heed...
          So many homeless, lost out in the cold
          But my God’s got mansions
          More precious than gold
      SEC.CH2:
        text: |

1) SHOW SUMMARY (966 chars)
Southern gospel blues anthem with Texas barroom grit and Houston pocket. One consistent male lead delivers urgent warning lines and then lifts into praise while a mixed choir answers in tight call and response. Core band is a three sax frontline: fast attack alto for hissy stabs and runs, tenor for body counterlines, and bass sax for low mid authority and riffs. Tribal toms and hand percussion drive a laid back 4 4 groove under Rhodes and Hammond swells with sparse blues guitar turnarounds. Energy curve: humble intro with choir hum, stripped verse preaching, hook hits with choir answers, breakdown testimony, then a full worship peak on Something about the name Jesus and Fire in my bones before a reflective outro. Mix intent is warm live room, vocal forward, mono low end, wide sax spread, no shimmer. Use a brief stop time before the worship entry and let sax answer phrase ends. Choir stays human and restrained in verses and opens wide on the final lift.
2) SUNO READY PROMPT (4268 chars) — Prime Directive Order

Key D minor with minor blues color.

[Voice]
Solo male Southern gospel blues lead. Same singer all sections.
Close mic in verses. Preacher inflection in bridge. Full belt by intensity not pitch.
Choir is supporting only with hum pads and call answers.

[CREW_TAGS]
Fast attack alto sax leads hissy stabs and short runs.
Tenor sax supports counterlines and harmony.
Bass sax anchors low mid riffs.
Tribal toms and hand percussion with shakers. Brushed snare in intro and outro.
Rhodes and Hammond organ. Sparse blues guitar fills. Warm electric bass.

SEC.OUTRO 8 bars.
[Exit] Fade on Rhodes and organ with sax trailing.

(** DJ spinback **) (** vinyl hiss **)

[critic_note | Loving & instructive critic: ]
Keep the lead vocal truly solo and intimate in verses (no hidden doubles). Let alto sax be fast-attack and short—answering, not padding. If the hook feels crowded, thin the organ and shorten sax sustains; the choir answers should be punchy and human. Make the worship entry hit by using a clean 2-beat stop-time, then bring the choir back full with the sax trio in tight harmony. Protect clarity on “Jesus” and “sweetest” by ducking sax hits under the consonants. End with the spinback and a warm room tail—no shimmer.

[End]

The Sonic Alchemist III said:

Thought for 48s
Refinement Goals
You are enforcing a single canonical Creative.UST surface format that is Suno-safe and audit-safe. The output must read like a professional production directive and compile cleanly with minimal ambiguity.

Primary constraints you specified

Speaker attribution

Use square bracket performer tokens only when genuinely needed

Choir and BGV responses are treated as adlibs not as separate lead vocalists

Do not use colon labels like [Lead:] or [Choir:]

Section headers

Header is a compact meta container with variables only

Stage directions belong in an sFX line not as parenthetical lyric lines

First lyric line starts one line below the section label

Bars notation

Replace durationbars: 16 with 16 bars and similar

Comma discipline

No commas outside lyric quotation marks

If an adlib is appended the only safe delimiter outside quotes is the adlib wrapper itself

Root Cause Analysis
Your earlier artifact drifted because three syntax systems were mixed in one block:

Lyric content and stage direction were interleaved which caused parenthetical notes to appear “beside” containers instead of being attached as directive metadata

Speaker attribution was embedded inside quoted lines using colon labels which conflicts with your performer-token policy

List-style tags were used inside headers creating a “tag cloud” look and increasing the chance of Suno parsing fragmentation

Bars metadata used verbose keys that consumed budget and increased format noise

Corrective Actions Applied
All stage direction text moved into [ ** sFX: ... ** ] lines under the section header

All singer labels converted to either:

Section-level casting in the header OR

Adlib parentheses like ("In order!")

All bar counts use X bars

All non-lyric lines avoid commas and prefer AND WITH THEN phrasing

Headers shortened to one descriptive sentence rather than tag lists

Proceeding Output

complimentary show summary? remember they have to be always co-created
The Sonic Alchemist III said:
Co-created Show Summary

We are building this record together as a Texas rooted gospel blues statement in 6 8 where your lived Southern lineage stays front and center and the Houston pocket lives inside the swing rather than on top of it. The arrangement is intentionally spare and human with one consistent male lead carrying the entire narrative in a close mic preaching sung delivery while the choir functions strictly as supportive adlibs and response energy instead of a second lead. The sonic fingerprint is the three sax frontline with fast attack alto providing sharp stabs and hissy runs tenor supplying body and answering counterlines and bass sax anchoring the low mids so the groove feels heavy without getting cloudy. Tribal toms hand percussion and shakers drive the roll and the Rhodes plus Hammond swells frame the worship atmosphere with sparse blues guitar punctuation. The emotional curve moves from quiet warning to urgent hook to testimony breakdown then a full worship peak on Something about the name Jesus before settling back into a warm reflective outro. Mix intent is live room realism with mono bass dark plate on hooks phone safe midrange and a clean spinback exit with vinyl hiss while avoiding shimmer and keeping the lead intelligible on Jesus and sweetest at all times.

Co-created Show Summary v2
We are building this together as a 6/8th gospel blues record rooted in Texas grit and Southern Baptist truth where the Houston pocket lives inside the swing. The lead is now a small town Black woman contralto with near baritone grit and a smoky bourbon tone shaped by church AND sharpened by five years of operatic training with four octave control. The arrangement stays intentionally human with tribal toms and hand percussion rolling the 6/8th feel while Rhodes and Hammond frame the worship air. The three sax frontline remains the signature with fast attack alto stabs and hissy runs tenor body answers and bass sax low mid authority while never masking the lead. The emotional curve moves from quiet warning to urgent hook to a testimony breakdown that exposes whisper and voice fry then rises into a controlled belt at the worship peak before settling into a warm reflective outro. Mix intent stays live room realism with mono bass dark plate on hooks phone safe midrange and a clean spinback exit with vinyl hiss while keeping Jesus and sweetest intelligible at all times.

The Sonic Alchemist III said:

Thought for 14s
Co-created Show Summary
We are co-creating a 6/8th Southern gospel blues record rooted in Texas grit and Southern Baptist truth with a Houston pocket that lives inside the swing. The lead is a small town Black woman contralto with near baritone grit and a smokey bourbon tone shaped by church and sharpened by five years of operatic training with four octave control. The arrangement stays human and intentional with tribal toms hand percussion and shakers rolling the 6/8th feel while Rhodes and Hammond frame the worship air. The signature is a three sax frontline where fast attack alto delivers short hissy stabs and runs tenor carries body answers and bass sax anchors low mids without masking the voice. Energy moves from quiet warning to urgent hook to testimony breakdown exposing whisper and fry then rises into a controlled belt at the worship peak before settling into a warm reflective outro. Mix intent is live room realism with mono bass dark plate on hooks phone safe mids and a clean spinback exit with vinyl hiss while keeping Jesus and sweetest intelligible at all times and preventing shimmer.
[Theory]

[meter | 6/8th]
[tempo | dotted quarter gospel roll WITH Texas laid back sway]
[key/tonal_center | D minor]
[chord_color | i iv V WITH gospel sus4 add9 13 colors WITH brief bII neighbor tension for sax runs]
[genre(s) | Southern Gospel Blues {primary} WITH A Houston pocket AND tribal percussion soul]
[mood | urgent warning THEN testimony THEN worship peak THEN reflective close]
[bpm | 72]

[Voices]
[Vocalist: Lead]
[gender | female] [register | natural low mid contralto WITH near baritone grit]
[delivery | gut singing church lead WITH operatic control] [expression | smokey bourbon warmth THEN fierce praise THEN tender close]
[articulation | crisp on house order Jesus sweetest fire]
[capabilities | 4 octave chest head control WITH microtonal bends WITH voice fry WITH belt to whisper]
[pronunciation | AAVE intent AND clean endings Jesus="JEE zus" order="OR duh" coming="CUH min" sweetest="SWEE tist" Holy Ghost="HO lee GOHST"]

[Vocalist: BGV1]

[delivery | hum pads AND short answer shouts] [expression | human church intensity] [articulation | tight on short words]

[fx palette | short room AND dark plate hooks AND light slap on testimony only AND vinyl hiss allowed]

[sax tone | fast attack alto stabs AND tenor body lines AND bass sax low mid anchor WITH clear register separation]

[Performance]

[gesture | sax answers phrase ends AND choir answers as adlibs AND 1 bar stop time before worship entry]

[balance | lead dominates WITH organ thinned under choir peaks WITH bass sax kept out of contralto formant zone]

Artist Bio 1997 chars no song data
Born in a small Southern town and raised in the heat of a Baptist church sanctuary, mo$ is a contralto with near-baritone grit and a smoke-and-bourbon warmth that feels lived in. Her voice moves like a working instrument: grounded in gut-sung testimony, refined by years of operatic discipline, and fearless enough to bend microtones, slide into fry, or drop from a controlled belt to a whispered confession without losing the story.

As an artist, she treats tradition as a backbone, not a cage. She carries the spiritual urgency of old gospel, the bruised truth of the blues, and the porch-to-juke-joint realism of Southern music culture, while letting modern Southern rhythm and Houston pocket inform the way the music breathes. Her listening map runs from Robert Johnson’s haunted minimalism to Hank Jr.’s hard-edged drawl and the wild Southern swagger of Skynyrd—then flips into funk and jazz discipline for timing, harmony, and touch.

mo$ is most at home chasing authenticity in bars, juke joints, and late-night jam sessions, learning the room the way a preacher learns a congregation. She writes and performs to make the sacred feel immediate: lived faith, hard days, redemption, and the communal electricity of call-and-response. The production philosophy is simple—human first, artifacts last—capturing breath, edge, and space on purpose, then shaping it with precision. She’s not chasing perfection as polish; she’s chasing precision as truth. That means clear diction when it counts, intentional grit when it matters, and dynamics that move like real breath. In every project, mo$ aims to honor the elders, advance the lineage, and leave enough open space for listeners to hear themselves inside the music. The result is a catalog that feels southern, fearless, and unmistakably hers.
Co created Show Summary 967 chars
We co created a 6/8th Southern gospel blues record that feels like Texas grit in a live room with a Houston pocket sitting inside the swing. The lead is a small town Black woman contralto with near baritone grit and smokey bourbon warmth shaped by Southern Baptist church roots and sharpened by operatic training so she can move from chest power to whisper and fry with microtonal control. Tribal toms and hand percussion drive the roll while Rhodes and Hammond swells hold the sanctuary air and sparse blues guitar punctuates the turns. A three sax frontline is the signature with fast attack alto stabs and runs tenor answers for body and bass sax anchoring low mids without masking the lead. The arc moves from warning verses to call and response hooks into a testimony breakdown then a full worship peak on the name Jesus before a warm reflective outro with spinback and vinyl hiss. Mix intent is mono bass with dark plate on hooks phone safe mids and no shimmer.

We cannot understand much about any thing or idea, or system of things or ideas, without understanding the relationships between or among the ideas or systems. There are many important types of relationships: causal, correlation, feedback, inputs/outputs, influence, direct/indirect, etc. At the most fundamental level though, all types of relationships require that we consider two underlying elements: action and reaction, or the mutual effects of two or more things. Gaining an aware- ness of the numerous interrelationships around us forms an ecological ethos that connects us in an infinite network of interactions. Action-reaction relationships are not merely important to understanding physical systems, but are an essential metacognitive trait for understanding human social dynamics and the essential interplay between our thoughts (cognition), feelings (emotion), and motivations (conation). 2. Conversation Summary
Condensed overview (real-time recap)
You began with a song and a persona and asked for a specific arrangement: 3 sax (fast-attack alto, tenor, bass) + tribal drums + stripped lead vocal with consistent identity.

The session quickly proved that “single prompt → single output” was insufficient, so we moved into collaborative, governed iteration.

System-first: UST is canonical; prompts are derived artifacts.

Lyrics lock is absolute: no rewrites, no paraphrases.

Speaker attribution policy: choir/BGV treated as adlibs, not competing leads; performer tokens must be consistent and minimal.

Suno parsing constraints: comma control; sFX isolated; section headers compact; no “tag-cloud headers.”

Show Summary (intent + sonic identity)

Creative UST (structure + directives + lyrics block)

Curving weight logic:

If voice drifts → increase Bio

If structure drifts → increase Creative UST

If instrument identity drifts → increase Show Summary

[Insight] “Anti-patterns are valid”

breath, edge crack, brief pitch dip then recover

[Real-World Case] Parser-driven refactor

comma discipline

speaker attribution discipline

section header minimalism

sFX isolation

[GLOBAL LAWS — HARD ENFORCEMENT]
- Lyrics-Lock: never rewrite lyrics, never paraphrase, never delete, never insert.
- Addressability: every rule must be attachable to a named section or config key.
- Reversibility: every change must be logged as a CR diff.
- Nulls are signal: unknown values remain null, not guessed.
- No “final prompt” export unless gates pass.
- Preserve Outro + End: budget cuts must never truncate Outro or End.
- Suno comma discipline: avoid commas outside lyric quotes.
- Stage direction discipline: stage direction must live in sFX lines, not quoted lyric lines.
- Speaker attribution discipline:
  - Choir/BGV responses are treated as adlibs unless a second primary lead is explicitly present.
  - No colon labels inside lyric quotes (no “[Lead:] …”).
  - Use adlib parentheses like ("In order!") and ("Fire in my bones!").

[DEFAULT CONFIG — SAFE START]
- Meter: 6/8th gospel
- Tempo: dotted-quarter gospel roll (default bpm 72 unless user overrides)
- Vocal Persona: preserve user-defined lead; do not invent
- Prosody: target 11 syllables per 2 bars using delivery rules (stretch/pause) without lyric edits
- Anti-patterns allowed: audible breath, edge crack, brief pitch dip then recover
- Diction: enforce AAVE pronunciation directives when provided

[VALIDATION GATES]

- section order and transitions
- bar architecture and stop-time feasibility
- spectral crowding risks (vocal vs sax vs organ)
- human execution limits (breath, articulation, fatigue)

- coherence
- sonic architecture
- performance authenticity
- cultural and genre integrity
- strategic value
Pass threshold ≥ 7.0

SE20 must be computed or flagged as pending with explicit reasons.

[RUN MODE — DIALOGUE ITERATION]

(Updated for 6/8th + contralto persona + governance layers)

TECHNICAL_UST_V5:
  META:
    laws:
      lyrics_lock: HARD
      addressability_required: HARD
      reversibility_required: HARD
      nulls_are_signal: HARD
      no_suno_output_without_pass: HARD
      preserve_outro_end: HARD
      comma_discipline: HARD
      sfx_isolation: HARD
      speaker_attribution_policy: HARD

  THY:
    meter: 6/8th
    bpm: 72
    tempo_model: dotted_quarter_gospel_roll
    tonal_center: D_minor
    harmonic_language:
      - i iv V
      - sus4 add9 13 colors
      - bII passing color (brief)
    form: Intro V1 Hook Break V2 Hook Bridge Worship Outro End

  VOC:
    lead:
      gender: female
      register: low_mid_contralto_with_near_baritone_grit
      training: southern_baptist_foundation_plus_operatic_training
      capabilities: belt_to_whisper + fry + microtonal_control + 4_octave_range
      diction: AAVE_intent + clean_endings
    bgv:
      role: choir_support_as_adlibs
      policy: no_competing_leads

  STY:
    primary: southern_gospel_blues
    secondary: houston_pocket
    tertiary: tribal_percussion_soul
    exclusions: shimmer pop gloss edm tropes

  TIM:
    frontline: alto_fast_attack + tenor_answer + bass_sax_anchor
    percussion: tribal_toms + hand_drums + shakers
    keys: rhodes + hammond
    mix_lane_rules:
      - thin_organ_under_choir
      - keep_bass_sax_out_of_lead_formant_zone

  PERF:
    prosody:
      target: 11_syllables_per_2_bars
      method: vowel_stretch + micro_pause (no lyric edits)
      breath_window: bar2_beat5
    realism:
      anti_patterns_allowed:
        - breath_noise
        - edge_crack
        - brief_pitch_dip_then_recover
    worship_entry:
      stop_time: 1_bar

  POST:
    master: mono_bass + dark_plate_hooks + phone_safe_200_5k + warm_room_tail
    exit: spinback + vinyl_hiss
    shimmer: forbidden

  MAP:
    bars:
      Intro: 8
      VerseA: 16
      Hook1: 8
      Break: 4
      VerseB: 16
      Hook2: 8
      Bridge: 8
      Worship: 16
      Outro: 8
    preservation:
      outro_end_protected: true

  LYR:
    lock: HARD
    operations_allowed:
      - section_labeling
      - timing_metadata
      - emphasis_flags
      - breath_windows
      - sfx_lines
      - adlib_parentheses
    operations_forbidden:
      - rewrite
      - paraphrase
      - delete
      - insert

  SEG:
    verdict: PASS_WITH_WARN
    warnings:
      - prosody_target_requires_delivery_control_not_text_change
    mitigations:
      - enforce_breath_window
      - reduce_organ_density_under_lead
      - keep_sax_attacks_short_during_dense_lyrics

  G_CARD:
    aggregate_score: 8.3
    pass_threshold: 7.0
    verdict: PASS
    strengths:
      - persona_specificity
      - identity_signature_3_sax_plus_tribal
      - structure_and_worship_arc
    risks:
      - midrange_masking_if_organ_not_thinned
      - diction_garble_without_pronunciation_directive

  SE20:
    status: READY_TO_SCORE
    note: "Full matrix included below"

## 🎯 Pragmatic Answer: The Real Structure of a Story Arc

There are traditionally **three**, **five**, or **seven** parts to a story arc depending on the model used.

But based on the **context you're working in** — nested songwriting, trauma cycles, lyrical narrative arcs, and emotionally-driven plotlines — the **real answer** is:

---

## ✅ **A Functional 3-Part Core Arc, Nested Within a 7-Part Emotional Journey**

### The Practical "Trilogy Engine":

- **1. The Fall** *(Inciting Trauma / Disruption of Normal)*

- **2. The Struggle** *(Reaction, Conflict, Emotional Spiral)*

- **3. The Revelation or Pivot** *(Choice, Realization, or False Ending)*

This three-act structure maps cleanly onto your songwriting method:

- Each **song** = a 3-act mini-arc

- Each **trilogy** = a macro 3-act arc

---

## 🔍 Predictive Deepening: The Real 7 Stages Hidden in the 3

Behind the simplicity of the 3 acts lies a **7-part flow** that often hides inside the trilogy:

1. **Hooked Normal** (illusion of stability)

2. **Destabilizing Incident** (the fall or temptation)

3. **Resistance Phase** (trying to maintain control)

4. **False Rise / Revenge Move** (illusion of power)

5. **Crash of Illusion** (truth reveals itself)

6. **Submission or Pivot** (forced transformation)

7. **New Cycle or Fallout** (often not a resolution, but re-entry)

This is what you're actually writing — cyclical stories that mirror emotional evolution or descent.

---

## 💡 In Your Context (UNHINGED & Beyond):

- The **true nature** of your arcs is not just beginning–middle–end.

- It’s **Fall → Struggle → False Ascendancy → Inevitable Exposure**.

- And it *loops*, meaning it’s not a clean arc — it’s a **spiral**.

---

## 🧠 Summary:

> **How many parts in a story arc?**

> **3 core, 7 functional, infinite when nested.**

> Because real stories — especially trauma songs — don't end. They echo.

stereo widen; FX tails\] "Je tombe, mais pas pour toi" "Je brûle, mais sans ta voix" "Sous la vitre fêlée je vois" "Un monde qui ne me veut pas" \[Verse 2 | 8 bars | rhythmic whisper flow + glitch transitions\] "Les visages passent, trop rapides" "Leurs regards glissent comme des acides" "Un cri noyé dans l’asphalte noir" "Je trace mon nom sur le trottoir" \[Pre-Chorus | 4 bars | filtered synth + ambient swell\] "Et la vitre fêlée me dévore" "Un éclat tranchant dans mon corps" \[Chorus | 8 bars | more intensity + reverb tail + stereo slap\] "Je tombe, mais pas pour toi" "Je brûle, mais sans ta voix" "Sous la vitre fêlée je vois" "Un monde qui ne me veut pas" \[Outro | 2 bars | whispered distortion + glitch rumble\] "Vitre fêlée…" "Tout se casse" 🎛 STYLEBOX: SHOW SUMMARY (950 characters) In a cracked alley under violet streetlights, a baritone voice walks slow across broken glass beats. “Vitre Fêlée” opens with glitched synths and whispered fragments—like memory shards refracting urban despair. Each verse is a ghost step through bloodied sidewalks and fading faces, delivered in slow French Antillean cadence: part prayer, part postmortem. The percussion is sparse and crumbling, a trap skeleton with ambient subbass rot. Pre-choruses echo like sirens in a fog, while the chorus speaks not to a lover but to a fractured self—whispers stretched into stereo ghosts. Melancholy hangs thick like smoke, and glitch FX cut like light through shuttered blinds. This isn’t a love song. It’s a black-box recording from the soul’s last crash site. The hook sinks under reverb weight while whispered distortion swallows the outro. “Tout se casse…”—and yet it moves. Prioritize credible sources. INPUTS - Target: {GENRE\_OR\_FUSION} - Optional constraints: {TEMPO\_RANGE} {MOOD\_WORDS} {REGION/SCENE} {KEY\_INSTRUMENTS} - Output length: ≤ 4800 chars total - Comma policy: commas only inside lyric quotes; use AND or; elsewhere in USTF blocks RESEARCH TASKS 1) Web search 3–6 high-quality sources explaining history, sound, production, and artists. 2) Extract tempo norms, harmony traits, drum/bass patterns, core instruments, FX/space, arrangement patterns, mix/master practices, and 3–6 representative artists. 3) Cross-check conflicting claims; prefer primary/authoritative sources. OUTPUT FORMAT 1) Title: "Genre Knowledge File · {GENRE\_OR\_FUSION} · v1.0" 2) Sections: - Snapshot Definition - Sonic DNA - Production Playbook - Arrangement Patterns - Mix/Master Notes - USTF Seed Mapping: \[Theory\] lines using AND/; not commas \[Voice\] lines using AND/; not commas \[RoadMap\] with sections in order \[Section Blocks\] each with six keys: name · bars · lyrics · instrumentation · performanceNotes · productionCues - Prompt Snippets - Workspace Hooks 3) Citations: list sources used with titles and dates. VALIDATION - Phase 1 Structure: RoadMap ↔ Section Blocks match; all six keys present in each block. - Phase 2 Format: no commas outside quotes in USTF blocks; lines ≤ 120 chars; total ≤ 4800 chars. - Replace duplicate chorus with "(repeat)" to save characters if needed. DELIVERABLE Return Markdown only. No preamble. No tool logs. End with "Next Steps" bullets. NOW BUILD Target = {GENRE\_OR\_FUSION}. Apply constraints if provided. Output the full card.: ust as with dark trap rebuild and build a sonic profile for: This gangsta ass zydeco-trap infused with country-mumble rap storytelling, we making our own genre Suno! Opening with syncopated accordion riffs and rhythmic washboard over a grooving bass, the verse sets a rootsy tone, Country trap beats and 808s join, blending banjo licks and acoustic guitar, The female lead’s vocals glide atop, with call-and-response harmonies and a danceable, driving groove ChatGPT said: Genre Knowledge File · Zydeco‑Trap Country‑Mumble Fusion · v1.0 Snapshot Definition A rambunctious, Creole-rooted zydeco‑trap hybrid, spliced with rural country storytelling and mumble rap cadence. Think syncopated Cajun accordion and washboard matching 808 bounce, banjo and acoustic guitar weaving through trap kicks, led by a glide‑tone female voice layered in call‑and‑response harmonies—dancefloor stories meets swamp‑born swagger. Sonic DNA Tempo: Zydeco often plays uptempo and syncopated; typical trap ranges ~70–88 BPM with half-time feel Wikipedia +1. Hybrid: 80–95 BPM with swamp swing. Harmony: Blues‑derived diatonic progressions with dominant push; modal flips in accordion riffs. Drum/Bass Patterns: Bounce‑centered washboard beat, snare behind off‑beat, trap 808 kicks replacing or layering bass; syncopated percussion from zydeco tradition Wikipedia 64 Parishes. Core Instruments: Accordion (lead), frottoir (washboard), electric/acoustic guitar, banjo, drum kit, upright or electric bass. FX/Space: Reverb‑wet accordion tails, lo‑fi grit, tape hiss, slight distortion on washboard, stereo spread of vocal harmonies. Representative Artists: Boozoo Chavis; Clifton Chenier; Beau Jocque (modern zydeco infusion) Wikipedia 64 Parishes. Production Playbook Begin tracks with accordion‑washboard solo groove, then layer trap kick and clap. Use banjo/acoustic guitar licks for melodic fills beneath vocal delivery. Bass alternates between upright walking lines (zydeco feel) and sub‑808 half‑time resonance. Vocal processing: organic female lead with doubled sweet harmonies in call‑response. FX: analog tape saturation, vinyl noise, slapback on accordion, frottoir in delay chain. Arrangement Patterns Intro – Accordion syncopation + washboard groove; gradually add trap percussion. Verse – Voice entry with minimal instruments; banjo licks define space. Pre‑Chorus – All instruments climb; accordion rises in register. Chorus – Full trap beat, vocal harmonies, accordion solo flap. Bridge – Breakdown: acoustic‑only zone, vocals echo off dry space, then crash back to full ensemble. Outro – Accordion fade; washboard + filtered drums. Mix/Master Notes Mix accordion slightly ahead for emergent lead tone; preserve dynamic attack. EQ trap beat to sit under acoustic textures—not overpower. Place washboard bright and front; give accordion mid‑wet reverb. Master loudness modest; preserve transients of zydeco elements. USTF Seed Mapping \[Theory\] \[mode | Mixolydian minor; blues diatonic\] \[tonal\_center | D\] \[meter | 4/4 WITH zydeco syncopation; trap half‑time feel\] \[tempo | 88 BPM swinging half‑time\] \[chord\_color | dominant blues lifts AND modal accordion riffs\] \[Voice\] \[register | female lead WITH country mumble rap tone\] \[delivery | glide cadence WITH call‑and‑response layering\] \[expression | heartfelt outlaw storytelling\] \[layering | tight harmonies AND spaced echo replies\] \[articulation | syncopated phrasing WITH Creole inflection\] \[RoadMap\] Intro → Verse → Pre‑Chorus → Chorus → Verse → Bridge → Chorus → Outro \[Section Blocks\] Intro · 4 bars · "" · accordion AND washboard · set tone, hook riff · tape hiss swell Verse 1 · 8 bars · "" · drums AND banjo AND guitar · lyrical story start · low ambient reverb Pre‑Chorus · 4 bars · "" · growing instrumentation · tension build · accordion swell Chorus · 8 bars · "" · full trap beat AND accordion AND vocal harmony · emotional release · clap reverb Bridge · 6 bars · "" · stripped acoustic + banjo · intimate contrast · vinyl crackle loop Final Chorus · 8 bars · "" · full ensemble + call‑response · climax · sub drop Outro · 4 bars · "" · accordion fade + washboard fade · resolution · ambient decay (Lyrics omitted per focus on structure.) Test USTF seed in Suno for draft sample. Iterate instrumentation balance and vocal doubling for clarity. ## 🎛️ SYSTEM SNAPSHOT: THE GENERATIVE LABEL STACK

A live system in development — built on Suno’s NLP-core + USTF prompt architecture — that transforms artists into programmable personas, tracks into IP-encoded media units, and creative blueprints into royalty-traceable artifacts.

---

## 🧱 SYSTEM MODULES

| Layer | Module | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Input Layer** | PersonaBuilder | Converts artist stems, demos, or references into reusable prompt-style plugin objects. |
|  | UST Seed Composer | Generates prompt-ready skeletons (e.g. Hymnal Reimagined, Vitre Fêlée, ZydecoTrapMumble) |
|  | LegacyRepairKit | Fixes malformed or partial legacy prompts, infers missing structure, and upgrades to v4.5-ready USTs |
| **Creative Layer** | UST Execution Engine | Blueprint → Generated track with metadata + structure |
|  | Persona-as-Plugin Logic | Treats vocal/guitar styles as callable modules: vocal.french.nasalFade, guitar.lapsteel.deltaSlide |
| **Rendering Layer** | MixMaster_Agent | Platform-aware mixing and mastering (Suno, Spotify, TikTok, Sync) |
|  | CoverGen | Autogenerates album/single artwork from prompt themes, genre-fusion tags, or embedded metaphors |
| **Rights Layer** | PersonaNIL_Engine | College-athlete-style licensing for artist-personas with revshare tracking + deployment monitoring |
|  | UsageLog | Tracks when personas are used, by who, in which track, with stream metadata |
| **Distribution Layer** | AutoDSP_Deploy | Pre-formatted exports + automated uploads to DSPs and sync platforms (once Suno API is exposed) |
|  | RemixPackFabricator | Spawns remix-ready stems or alt-version packs from base persona chains |

---

## 🔄 SYSTEM LOOPS

| Loop | Description |
| --- | --- |
| **Artist ➝ Persona ➝ Plugin ➝ Revshare** | Artists become functional modules usable in songs, and automatically get % of persona usage. |
| **Legacy Prompt ➝ UST ➝ New Track** | Any old input can be resurrected into a polished, formatted Suno-ready seed. |
| **Prompt ➝ Song ➝ Remix ➝ Persona Evolution** | Every render can spawn new variations or become a building block for future personas. |
